uh fun fact the normal candles most traders use are also a Japanese candles They were very early in the development of technical analysis But this is a Japanese charting style that smooths out the candles And this is very important and you need to have this in mind throughout the rest of the video And the goal of hikenastic charts is to basically cut out the noise of normal candles so you can the hyenashi is the average of all of these points And the open of the hyenashi is the midpoint of the last hyenashi candle And don't worry I will jump into trading view very soon and show you guys this uh visually because it's much easier to understand But the high and low here are still uh the extreme points of the current bar So this is the same thing as with the normal candle So in simple fully contained within the range So within the highest and lowest point of the first candle The first candle is called the mother uh bar So if we have a candle let's say that this right here is the mother candle And then we might have a candle that looks something like this as the second candle You can actually see that this second candle is completely contained within the range of the first bar so throughout this course we will give every single chart pattern both a direction and a type so let's take a look at both of these classifications individually the direction specifies how the price will move after the chart pattern has appeared and we have two different directions a chart pattern can be either bullish which indicates that the price will go up after the chart pattern the other direction is bearish and indicates that the price will go down  
after the chart pattern has appeared not every single bullish chart pattern will lead to the price going up and not every single bearish chart pattern will lead to the price going down but if we can learn both how to identify and how to trade these patterns correctly this will be true most of the time the other classification is the type and the type specifies the price Direction after compared to the price Direction before  
and once again here we have two main types the first one is the reversal and the second type is the continuation and we have two different types of reversals the first reversal is the bullish reversal and for the bullish reversal before the pattern appears we have the price going down then the pattern appear so this circle right here is the chart pattern and then after the pattern the  
price goes up the other type of reversal is the bearish reversal and this is the opposite of the bullish one so before the chart pattern appears we see the price going up the pattern appears and then after the pattern the price is going down but we also have two different types of continuation patterns the first continuation pattern is the bullish continuation and this means that before the pattern appears the price is going up then the chart pattern appears  
but after the pattern the price will continue to go up that is why it's called continuation pattern and of course the opposite is true for the bearish continuation for this one the price goes down before the pattern appears then the pattern appears and after the pattern the price continues to go down but we also have one more type this right here is the type that I call uncertain and these patterns are  
very important to be able to identify because these patterns will not have a clear Direction and many times they will also not have a clear type so these patterns can be super hard to trade and most of the times these are patterns that I try to avoid and now you may wonder why do I even include uncertain patterns in this course well when it comes to trading it's of course important to be able to spot trading opportunities but it's actually also

pattern so this is where the price goes down we have the pattern and then the price goes up another example could be a bearish continuation pattern this would be the price going down the pattern appears and then the price continues to go down yet another example could be a bearish reversal in this case we have the price going up before the pattern then the pattern appears and then the price goes down so now guys we are finally ready to start exploring the chart patterns and for

which means that the price is going down before the pattern appears and the goal with this pattern is to reverse the price to the upside so to identify the double bottom you first need to identify a downtrend this is when we are printing both lower highs and also lower lows after the move towards the downside what you're looking out for here is two lows which are at around the same level these two lows doesn't have to be on the exact same level but they should be  
roughly at the same price what we usually do here is that we drag a horizontal line between the two lows and we also want to draw a horizontal line connecting the high here in between the two low points this move right here the first move down after the pattern has appeared is also significant so now let's take a look at how do we trade this pattern well first of all you can see that this is a double bottom because we had a move towards the downside we saw one and two  
lows at the same level and then a reversal towards the upside the most common entry point is to enter on the candle that breaks above the line defined by the high in between these two low points however I actually have another entry point that I personally prefer many times but first take a look at the strategy for entering the breakout so your entry would be where this Candlestick close so at the high of the close right here if you enter  
at that point the most common place to set your stop loss is to set it just below the lowest point of the double bottom and if you don't know the stop- loss is where you will automatically sell if the price goes down to this point as for your Target level what I usually recommend here is to Target a risk to reward ratio of 1 to two or more and what I mean with this is that the distance from your entry point to your target  
should be more than twice as long as your distance down here to your stop loss which would make your profit Target around here so that is one simple strategy however another strategy that I personally really like is to enter at the candle that just slightly dips below the low created by the first bounce what you can then do is that you can enter on the candle close of that move and one thing to notice is that you will not  
always see the price dip below the line created by this point uh many times the price will reverse just slightly above and then this is not as good of a strategy but when the price Wicks below this line right here I personally like this type of entry for this type of Entry I would also place my stop loss just below the lowest point of that candle and using a risk reward ratio of two perhaps our profit Target would be here so this right here is a much quick

iconic chart patterns and that is the head and shoulders pattern The Head and Shoulders pattern is a bearish reversal pattern so before the pattern we have an uptrend and after the pattern we have a downtrend the pattern itself consists of five important points first of all we have the left shoulder which is this point right here we have the head and we have the right shoulder the other two important points are  
these two low points that happens between the three high points or we actually have a sixth important Point as well and that is this point right here and later on I will talk about why that point is so important and the reason this is called a head and shoulders pattern is because as you can see the pattern kind of looks like the head the highest point in the head and then we have the left shoulder and the right shoulder and the pattern doesn't have to be straight like this it can sometimes slope a bit to to the right  
right slope a bit to the left but the important Point here is that we have a high then we have a higher high and then we have a lower high what you also want to do for the head and shoulders pattern is that you want to connect the two low points in between the highs so we want to draw a line in between this point and this point and another tip for you guys is that this pattern becomes stronger if this low point right here is actually lower  
than this low point right here and this is from a market structure perspective after watching this course I highly recommend that you guys check out my course on Market structure I will make sure to leave the link to that video up in the corner of this video and the reason for this is that if this point right here is lower compared to the previous low then you can see that we have both lower lows and at the same time we have lower highs so in this  
this case after this high right here we actually have the beginning of a downtrend because in order to have a downtrend we need to have lower highs and lower lows that is the beginning of a downtrend so having this point a little bit lower than the previous Point makes this pattern a little bit more likely to succeed so now let's take a look at the Head and Shoulder strategy and take a look at a real Market example so the first thing we of course need to do is to identify the pattern in this case you can see that we have  
one high then we have a higher high and then we have a lower high and what you also can see here is that the lows in between the highs are sloping downwards and as I said that makes the pattern stronger as for the entry points we have two main ways to enter the first entry point is this point right here you can see when the price breaks below the downward sloping line by the way this line right here is what we call  
the neckline if you use this entry point the most common place to set your stop- loss is to set it just above the right shoulder of the pattern so you set it just above this point right here and if you're using a 2 to one risk reward your profit Target will would be all the way down here so it is important to notice here that compared to the previous patterns we have been talking about the head and shoulders pattern is a very large pattern so it will take long time

and the upper line in the case of an uptrend need to have the exact same slope but now what about the different types of trend channels well first of all we have the ascending Channel this is a trend have reversals patterns signals potential reversals or continuation a reversal pattern right here is simply a a formation that indicates that the price is reversing from either down to up like right here so we might have down then the pattern appears and then it reverse up or the opposite we might have the price going up then a bearish reversal pattern appears indicating that the price will continue to go down while continuation patterns on on the other hand continuation patterns is the opposite it's when the price goes up a pattern appears and if this is a continuation pattern it indicates that the price will continue to go up or the opposite if we have the price going down the pattern appears uh the continuation pattern indicates that the price will continue to go down so we have reversal patterns and continuation patterns and these patterns work for multiple reasons uh for example as I said the patterns themselves tell us about the sort of psychology behind the buyers call an impulsive move then it pulls back and creates a low and in order to have an uptrend we need to not only have higher highs but we also need to have higher lows and the opposite is true for the downtrend we need to have lower highs and lower lows so a downtrend lower highs plus lower lows we also have something called a sideways trend uh another word for this is a trading range a sideways market and this is when the market has no no clear direction and is going sideways and another very important thing to notice right away is that trends exist on multiple time frames in fancy words we say that trends are fractal meaning that within every impulse move we also have small trends and within the pullbacks and impulse move uh on the next time frame we have small trends uh within that as moves over time so this is what we mean by dynamic and a downtrend line connects lower highs so if we have an uptrend that looks something like this the trend line is simply the line that connects the first we have a relatively sharp move in One Direction in this example we have a move towards the upside this move is usually called an Impulse or a trend leg following this move we tend to have a move in the opposite direction this move is not as sharp or as strong and this is basically where the market rests before the next impulse to the upside this move is what we call a pullback or retracement when we Mark the moves like I did right here for example you know

think about like a big Bank hedge fund or another actor with lots of capital and let's think about how these actors would execute their moves in the market well if you are let's say a bank and needs to buy a lot of something you could if you want to just buy everything at once but this would probably push up the price very very high up and that would make it you know unnecessarily expensive for you a smarter way to execute this trade  
is to buy a little bit you buy a little bit when you start to realize that there's not a lot of liquidity and that the market is going up a bit too fast then you may pause your buying for a bit and since you have pushed the price up this can often lead to the price you know going back a bit here sometimes the b2c move are actually more like a flat move but many times you will see the market going down and retracing a bit perhaps people that bought all the way down here sees this as a good opportunity to take

in trading ranges as well as some tips and tricks so yeah Guys Without further Ado let's Jump Right In all right then so what is a trading range or in other words a consolidation well from earlier in this course we know what an uptrend is we know the exact rules in order to have an uptrend we need to have consecutive higher lows and we also need to have consecutive higher highs we also know the exact rules for a downtrend in order to have a  
downtrend we need to have consecutive lower highs while at the same time we need to have consecutive lower lows so from this information it's easy to think about a trading range as a market where the highs here are at around the same level and the lows here are also at around the same level and this definition is not really wrong but at the same time it's too simplistic so for example this Market we see right here  
is indeed a trading range however it's very important to understand that we have many different types of trading ranges and depending on how a trading range look different types of trading ranges can give us different information about where the price is likely to go next so for example this trading range right here is what I would call either a box range or a clean trading range so a clean trading range is  
basically when the highs are at the around same level and the lows here are at the around same level but as you know from the chapter about uptrends and downtrends when we zoom in we will always be able to find shorter term Market structure within the longer term Market structure when we zoom into the shorter term time frame we will actually be able to see that we will find Trends within the trading range with that said in real Market  
it's actually very rare that we have these sort of perfect ranges most of the time when you look at real charts you will see trading ranges that looks more something like this you will see that the price action within the range will look super messy and after you have been trading for a while you will soon realize that it's often very hard to trade inside of a trading range or in other words the price action inside of a trading  
range so the price action right here will many times be highly random and when the price is highly random it basically means that it's super hard for us traders to make profit on a consistent basis and our job as Traders is to find moments in the markets when the price is less random because less Randomness means that we as Traders can make better predictions and as a result take better trades and now you may of course wonder if the price  
inside the range is highly random what should we as Traders focus on well pretty much all the trading strategies revolves around the edges of the trading range or in other words the resistance and the support and we can actually categorize pretty much all range trading strategies into two different categories we have support and resistance holding and support and resistance breaking  
for the support and resistance holding strategies the goal is pretty simple we look to either buy against support and expect the price to bounce and that way make money or short against resistance or in other words we expect the price to get reacted and make money by the price going down these two points you can see right here and right here where you see the price quickly either dipping below support or  
quickly pushing above resistance can many times be excellent entry points but as always the context here is very important so where on the chart this type of price move happens can shift the win rate either up or down and this type of pattern when the price either pushes below or quickly pushes above a support or resistance actually has a name and I will talk about this very soon when we talk about the W of Market cycle but for now you can just take a note that these  
are very important points the other type of strategy is support and resistance breaking and another word for this is breakouts breakouts are simply when we either push above resistance or push below support and these points can also be excellent trading opportunities but how can we know when a breakout is about to happen well now I want to take a look at two breakout formations that you need to know and the first pattern looks  
like this the trading range starts off normally here but after a while we can see that the lows here in the range are starting to push higher and higher so as you can see we have highs here at the around same level but at the same time we are seeing consecutive higher lows this right here is an indication that the buying pressure inside the range is increasing the  
buyers in the market are accepting higher and higher prices and this can shift the probabilities in favor of a breakout towards the upside if you are a little bit familiar with chart patterns you might notice that this right here looks like an ascending triangle and an ascending triangle is a bullish sh pattern indicating that the price will push to the upside but the amazing thing about Market structure is that you don't have to memorize  
specific chart patterns it's much better to learn about the underlying principles that indicates a successful breakout and here the principle is basically that increasing buying pressure within a trading range makes a breakout more likely to occur so for example we don't have to see a triangle in order for this uh principle to be true instead we could have a range that perhaps looks more something like this you can see here the trading range starts off pretty nor  
normally but after a while we can see that the price starts to push very close to the resistance the buyers in this market are accepting higher and higher prices and the buying pressure is increasing and this right here is at least as far as I know not a common chart pattern but this pretty much tells us the same thing as the ascending triangle this indicates increasing buying pressure and the increasing buying  
pressure indicates that the resistance is more likely to break and we are more likely to see a successful breakout so now when we have a better understanding of breakouts it will be much easier to understand the two different types of trading ranges and that is reversal ranges and continuation ranges and the basic principle here is pretty simple a reversal range is simply a trading range that leads to a  
a reversal and a continuation range is a trading range that leads to a continuation of the trend that happened before the range appeared and by understanding the market structure inside the trading range we can get hints about whether this is a reversal range or a continuation range so for example if the price in this range starts to move something like this would you say that this trading range is more likely a reversal range  
or a continuation range if you want to you can pause the video right here and drop a comment down below well since we're seeing consecutive lower highs here going into the support it indicates increasing selling pressure and that makes the support less likely to hold and more likely that we see a break down however if the price start to move something like this it indicates increasing buying pressure and this trading range right here is more likely to  
to lead to a continuation now I want to switch the attention to two very common but very often misunderstood types of trading ranges and I'm talking about the converging trading range and the expanding trading range so the converging trading range is pretty simple it's a range that is getting Tighter and Tighter so it's a range that looks something like this you can see in this case we have consecutive  
lower highs right while at the same time we're seeing consecutive higher lows the most common name for this type of pattern is a symmetrical triangle and you usually use sort of two trend lines like this to define the pattern and in most chart pattern textbooks you will find that uh this pattern is defined most of the times as a continuation pattern meaning that if we have an uptrend like we have right here before the pattern  
appears this pattern indicates a continuation to the upside uh but I have found that this patterns are actually much more tricky and in my opinion they can be very hard to trade because when you have been trading for a while you would realize that many times these patterns will actually lead to a continuation but you will also find that many times these patterns will actually lead to a reversal and also another very common thing is that these patterns  
leads to nothing they can just be the beginning of a trading range and after you will you will notice that many times what happens here is that we don't see a real breakout at all and the price just continues to go sideways in a random fashion and I think that one reason why these patterns are so hard to trade is because they don't tell us a lot about the buying and selling pressure because we have both lower highs and higher lows at the same times we can't really tell if the buyers or  
or sellers are in control here and in my opinion it can also be very hard to Define risks for this pattern SO trading breakouts from symmetrical triangles is at least something I personally uh try to avoid however guys I don't want to discourage you from Trading converging patterns for example I personally find that the rising wedge that looks something like this and the falling wedge that looks something like this can be very useful patterns but this type of triangle that is very symmetric

is much worse than the converging pattern this is a pattern I completely avoid because what this pattern at least for me tells me about the price is that the price is super confused a market that is going sideways while at the same time seeing increased volatility what this tells me is that no one really has control over the market and it's super super hard to trade so for example let's say that we actually see a break of this expanding range right here  
what you can notice is that when we see the break the price has already seen such a massive move so at that point at least for me it's super hard to try to Define some sort of risk to reward where should we set our Target at that point where should we set our stop loss so my personal recommendations for expanding ranges is to avoid them so yeah this is my personal opinion about expanding ranges but I'm very curious here if any of you guys

of who was this guy and why is his Market cycle still so relevant today after the short introduction we will learn the market cycle and we will talk about the four different phases of Market we have the accumulation phase we have the markup we have the distribution phase and we have the markdown and during this chapter we will of course talk about the technical aspects of wo's Market cycle but we will also  
also and this is very important we will also talk about the psychological aspects of the market cycle so let's dive in so Richard wof was one of the most influential figures in the world of technical analysis he started his career super early already at the age of 15 trading stocks and he basically dedicated his whole life in understanding markets and uncovering the secrets behind Market movements and one of his most famous works  
was his Market Cycle Theory and to this day wo's Market cycle is still a super useful Theory because this Theory allows small Traders like me and you to take advantage of the big investors like Banks and financial institutions by riding the waves of the big players we small Traders can benefit and make money the main Assumption of wo's Market cycle is that it is the Smart money that moves the market  
market so for example small Traders like you and me when we buy a stock or cryptocurrency we cannot really make the markets move but if you are a very large player like a bank or hedge fund you will need to buy or sell so much that you will actually cost the market to move so if we can learn how the big players think and how the big players move the market we can greatly improve our chances of making money and the market cycle consist of four  
different phases the first phase is called the accumulation phase and it's called the accumulation phase because this is a phase where smart money is accumulating or in other words buying assets originally the market cycle was developed for the stock market but it can be applied for you know cryptocurrencies and Forex as well and smart money's goal during the accumulation phase is basically to buy as much as they need without moving the price  
during this phase the smart money don't want to push the price up because they want to buy at cheap prices and because every time smart money buys they push up the price a bit they will need to spend a lot of time to accumulate their position for us small Traders this phase is often super boring because the price is only going sideways the media is not talking about the stock because the media and the public interest are often much more focused on you know stocks that either  
go up super fast or fall super fast so this whole accumulation phase is pretty much off the radar and that's exactly the goal of the smart money they want to buy without making any noise and without alerting the public during the accumulation phase the price will often be defined in between a support and a resistance area or in other words the market will often be range bound however in real markets this range is very seldom perfect many times  
s the price will go a bit above and many times the market will also quickly fall below the support and this dip right here below support in an accumulation phase actually has a name it's called a w of spring and springs can actually often be amazing trading opportunities because many times the Springs will be the absolute lows of downtrends so if you manage to buy a spring you can make some amazing profit  
the psychology behind the spring goes like this during the accumulation phase the smart money tends to buy at or near the support area however to test the strength of the market smart money might stop buying for a while to see if there's underlying buying pressure just below support sometimes the market will reverse up on its own and sometimes smart money will take this as an opportunity to buy even lower the second part of the cycle is the uptrend  
or markup and this phasee starts when the price breaks above the accumulation range however uptrends often start in a pretty sneaky and quiet way some traders that went short here during the resistance of the accumulation will help pushing the price up in the initial stage of the uptrend and some Traders will also buy the breakout but during the start of the uptrend the public and small investors are still mostly uninterested news outlets  
social media and so on and so on tends to focus much more on stocks that have been in an uptrend for a long while because the price traded at lower prices for such a long time you will actually more often than not hear many small Traders when the uptrend begins they will say things like it's way too expensive right now the fundamentals are not supporting this move and so on and so on so many smaller Traders will actually try to short the market and when Traders are  
short and the price start to go up once again they will have to buy back their stocks which leads to even more buying pressure when the price has been going up for a bit the public interest start to shift people will start talking about the market in the news on social media and so on and so on and many small Traders will buy on the hype which increases the buying pressure towards the upside however when people are the most bullish and everyone thinks the price would go up for  
ever this is usually when the distribution starts or in other words the end of the uptrend and according to wov the distribution starts because the smart money that bought all the way down here are now very happy with the new higher prices so they start to sell and once again smart money has so large positions so they can't just sell everything at once if they sold everything at once they would risk crashing the market  
Market completely so what they do instead is that they sell a little bit they wait for the pressure to go up once again then they sell a little bit they wait some more and then they sell a little bit they don't want to crash the market because they of course want to sell at as high prices as possible so the distribution phase can go on for a long period of time just as with the w of spring we also have the opposite of a w of spring and that is a w of  
of up thrust and that is a short period of time where the price spikes above the resistance of the distribution the up thrust happens because smart money pauses their selling for a while sometimes underlying selling pressure will come in and if the selling pressure doesn't come in smart money will often see this as very attractive prices to sell and push the price down once again the up thrust can often be amazing places to sell especially if you bought all the way down at the accumulation phase  
or if not they can be amazing shorting opportunities after the distribution phase has been going on for a long time small Traders and the media might start to lose a little bit of Interest they move on to the next hyped up stock and this can lead to the buying pressure decreasing when smart money realized that the underlying buying pressure is getting less and less they might sell a bit more aggressively which leads to even more selling pressure and this is often the  
the start of phase two which is the downtrend or the markdown the markdown is pretty much the opposite of the uptrend but with a few important differences one saying you might have heard is that the Bulls take the stairs while the bars takes the elevator and that is because the downtrend will often be more aggressive compared to the uptrend and one big reason for this is that fear is such a strong emotion so think about all the small Trad  
ERS that bought up here in the distribution phase remember that especially in the beginning of the distribution this was a phase when everyone was talking about the stock and so many small Traders bought so when the price start to fall here in the beginning small Traders might see this as an opportunity to buy more because the price right now is much cheaper compared to where the price traded for a long while during the distribution but as the price start to fall even deeper  
fear or even Panic might start to settle in and this can lead to the downtrend phase being very aggressive and actually let me know down in the comments below if you have noticed that movements towards the downside tend to be more aggressive than movements towards the upside but as you guys know nothing lasts forever when the price have fallen deep enough so that smart money once again sees this as attractive prices they will start buying  
again and this is when the accumulation phase starts again and the cycle repeats one thing that is important to notice here is that the cycle will not always be accumulation uptrend distribution downtrend uh sometimes you will have more something like this you you might have accumulation then an uptrend then accumulation once again and then yet another uptrend and so on and so on but now what you know both the psychological aspects and the technical as  
aspects about these faces you will hopefully be much better than an average Trader to detect what phase we are in right now and by knowing what phe we are in you can hopefully take better trading decisions all right so part three for this course is coming to an end soon if you have any questions about this video or this course as a whole I will try my very best to answer every single comment so make sure to drop a comment down below if you have any questions and also guys please let me know what you think here because I do think
